# New wording in Version 28, for the doctor to check (22 Oct 2026)

**Status, 23 Oct 2026:** Mohit: "doctor reviewed and approved". He sent this after being asked for the doctor's review of
sections 8 and 9 (and, before that, 6 to 9) ahead of the pilot. It's his word; the sign-off itself isn't in the repo.
Wording added after this note needs its own review.

Version 28 adds four features:
- **Iron tablets:** a daily tick, a reminder and help.
- **Free check-ups for you:** a Home card for women aged 30 to 65.
- **My ASHA or ANM:** her number, a one-tap call and a summary to show her.
- **Do it online:** the government's own websites.

Mohit's rule is that new health wording needs a doctor's review before it ships. So this file lists every new sentence a
woman will read, in English and Hindi. Lines already verified on 20 Oct are reused word for word, and section 2 names where
each one comes from. Only sections 1 and 3 need a fresh look.

## 1. New lines that touch health (please check)

The check-ups card restates the check-up list's own verified lines: "Once in 5 years from 30 to 65. VIA is free at your
Ayushman Arogya Mandir or PHC" and "A trained nurse or ANM checks both breasts and armpits by hand. Once in 5 years from 30
to 65. Free."

## 2. Verified lines reused word for word (no review needed)

**Iron tablets, the plans:**
- Pregnancy: from the article "The red tablet, the white tablet and two injections".
- After the birth: from "After birth: the first six weeks".
- The weekly tablet: from "Thyroid and anaemia tests".
- My doctor's dose: from the Anaemia page in Care.

**Iron tablets, "If it upsets you":**

| Line | Verified in |
|---|---|
| Take it two hours after a meal, with water or lemon water, not with tea or milk. If it upsets you, take it at bedtime. | The iron article |
| Never take calcium and the iron tablet together; each blocks the other. | The iron article |
| Constipation: more water, fruit, vegetables and walking. | The Anaemia page |
| Black stools are normal with iron. If the tablet upsets your stomach, tell the ASHA or doctor instead of stopping. | The "more iron" 21-day journey |
| Pause it during a fever, diarrhoea or other short illness, and restart when you are well. | The Anaemia page |

**Iron tablets, "When to get help":**

| Line | Verified in |
|---|---|
| Very pale, breathless on stairs, heart racing, or very tired … Below 7 needs a higher centre. | The iron article, "When to see a doctor" |
| Vomiting every tablet, or no rise in haemoglobin after 4 weeks of tablets: ask the ANM to refer you. | The iron article, "When to see a doctor" |
| Known thalassaemia or repeated blood transfusions … | The iron article, "When to see a doctor" |
| Severe vomiting, belly pain that does not settle, rash, swelling or trouble breathing: stop and see a doctor. | The Anaemia page |

**Stock:** "If the ANM runs out, ask her when stock comes; a Jan Aushadhi store may also have them cheaply." This is from
the iron article.

## 3. Facts on the "Do it online" page (please check, or have someone check)

Each line is from the scheme's official page, opened on 22 Oct 2026.

| Service | Line | Source |
|---|---|---|
| ABHA | A free 14-digit health ID … a doctor sees them only with your consent. You need: usually your Aadhaar and the mobile number linked to it, or ask at your Ayushman Arogya Mandir. | abha.abdm.gov.in; Sakhi's verified ABHA text |
| U-WIN | Your Td doses in pregnancy and your baby's vaccines, with certificates; a vaccination session near your PIN code. You need: a mobile number for the OTP. | uwin.mohfw.gov.in |
| PMMVY | ₹5,000 for the first child; apply on the portal or through the Anganwadi worker or ASHA; ₹6,000 for a second child who is a girl. You need: Aadhaar, an Aadhaar-linked bank or post office account, the MCP card, one eligibility proof. | pmmvy.wcd.gov.in FAQ |
| Ayushman card | Free hospital care of up to ₹5 lakh a year for the family; mobile and Aadhaar e-KYC; Ayushman Mitra desk; helpline 14555. | beneficiary.nha.gov.in; PIB |
| eSanjeevani | A government doctor on a video call, free; a mobile number for the OTP; timings depend on the state. | esanjeevani.mohfw.gov.in |
| Jan Aushadhi | Generic medicines at 50 to 80 percent lower prices, sanitary pads at ₹1 each. | janaushadhi.gov.in; Sakhi's verified "free government services" article |
| Warning | Never share an OTP with someone who calls you, and never pay anyone to get a scheme. | Safety advice, not health wording |

## 4. Interface text (no health content)

These need no clinical review:
- buttons: "I took it", "Took it", "Done", "Undo", "Later";
- counts: "Days taken", "Weeks taken", "{n} of 180 days";
- the reminder switches;
- "Her number stays on this phone. Sakhi never sends it anywhere.";
- "Don't know your ASHA? Ask at your nearest Anganwadi centre or Ayushman Arogya Mandir. In states that run it, 104 can help
  too.";
- the page titles.

## 5. Version 28, part 2: the pregnancy plan, scheme claims and the doctor report (please check)

Added the same day: My pregnancy plan (A1), My claims (B1), the report for the doctor (C1) and Care near me (B2, section
5.5). Care near me shows real places only once its data is bundled; see `docs/care-near-me-data-2026-10-22.md`.

### 5.1 The pregnancy plan: what shows when

Each card's text is the verified week-by-week tip for the week named in the last column, word for word. What is new is the
item's name and its window, which decides when it shows as "Due now". After the window it moves to "Not done yet", never to
an alarm.

| Item (English) | Hindi | Shows as due | Reminder | Card text: the verified tip of week |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Register your pregnancy | गर्भ का पंजीकरण | up to week 12 | none | 5 |
| Folic acid | फोलिक एसिड | up to week 12 | none | 4 |
| First check-up and free tests | पहली जाँच और मुफ़्त टेस्ट | up to week 12 | none (already due when she adds her pregnancy) | 9 |
| Td injections | Td के टीके | up to week 36 | Td-2, 4 weeks after Td-1 | 10 |
| Early scan, if advised | शुरुआती scan, अगर सलाह मिले | weeks 11 to 14 | none | 11 |
| Scheme money: PMMVY and JSY | योजना का पैसा: PMMVY और JSY | weeks 5 to 40 | none | 12 |
| Iron tablets | आयरन की गोलियाँ | weeks 13 to 40 | none | 14 |
| Calcium tablets | कैल्शियम की गोलियाँ | weeks 14 to 40 | none | 15 |
| Second check-up | दूसरी जाँच | weeks 14 to 26 | 10 am, first day | 16 |
| Anomaly scan | Anomaly scan | weeks 18 to 20 | 10 am, first day | 18 |
| PMSMA: free check-up on the 9th | PMSMA: 9 तारीख़ को मुफ़्त जाँच | weeks 13 to 40 | 9 am on each 9th | 23 |
| Sugar test (75 g) | शुगर टेस्ट (75 g) | weeks 24 to 28 | 10 am, first day | 24 |
| Third check-up | तीसरी जाँच | weeks 28 to 34 | 10 am, first day | 27 |
| Free birth at a government hospital (JSSK) | सरकारी अस्पताल में मुफ़्त डिलीवरी (JSSK) | weeks 28 to 40 | none | 29 |
| Your birth plan | आपका डिलीवरी प्लान | weeks 32 to 40 | none | 33 |
| ASHA and ambulance numbers | ASHA और एम्बुलेंस के नंबर | weeks 32 to 40 | none | 34 |
| Fourth check-up | चौथी जाँच | weeks 36 to 41 | 10 am, first day | 36 |
| Family planning after the birth | डिलीवरी के बाद परिवार नियोजन | weeks 36 to 40 | none | 39 |
| A check at 41 weeks | 41 हफ़्ते पर जाँच | weeks 41 to 42 (shown only from week 41) | none | 41 |

Please check:
1. **The windows.** The anomaly scan follows its verified tip (18 to 20 weeks). Should it run to 22 weeks? Should Td run up
   to week 36? Iron starts at week 13 ("from the fourth month"), calcium and PMSMA as shown.
2. **The item names**, especially "Early scan, if advised", "A check at 41 weeks" and "Family planning after the birth".
3. **What counts as done by itself:**
   - registration: a registration date in her MCP card;
   - each check-up: a check-up date inside its window;
   - Td: both doses, or a booster;
   - the sugar test: a 75 g result logged in weeks 24 to 28;
   - iron: an active iron plan;
   - ASHA numbers: her ASHA's number saved.

### 5.3 My claims (scheme pages)

| Where | English | Hindi |
|---|---|---|
| Button | Track my claim | मेरा दावा ट्रैक करें |
| Papers | Papers to keep ready. Tick each one you have. | तैयार रखने वाले काग़ज़। जो पास है, उसे टिक करें। |
| Steps | Getting papers ready · Applied · Money or card received | काग़ज़ तैयार कर रही हूँ · आवेदन किया · पैसा या कार्ड मिल गया |
| PMMVY deadline | Register by {date} (270 days after the birth) | {date} तक पंजीकरण करें |
| PMMVY reminder, a month and a week before | PMMVY: register soon | PMMVY: जल्द पंजीकरण करें |
| PMMVY, pregnant with two check-up dates | You have had two check-ups, so you can apply for the first part (₹3,000) now. | आपकी दो जाँचें हो चुकी हैं, इसलिए आप पहले हिस्से (₹3,000) के लिए अभी आवेदन कर सकती हैं। |
| Pregnancy list, new card | In 2025, families paid about ₹37,600 of their own money on average for a birth in a private hospital, and about ₹2,300 in a government one (national survey, NSS 80th round). Under JSSK, birth at a government hospital is free and cashless. | साल 2025 में परिवारों ने निजी अस्पताल में एक डिलीवरी पर औसतन लगभग ₹37,600 अपनी जेब से दिए, और सरकारी अस्पताल में लगभग ₹2,300 (राष्ट्रीय सर्वे, NSS 80वाँ दौर)। JSSK में सरकारी अस्पताल में डिलीवरी मुफ़्त और बिना नक़द के होती है। |

Sources, read on 22 Oct 2026:
- **Birth costs:** PIB's NSS 80th round note (January to December 2025), table B3-II. The average out-of-pocket spend per
  institutional birth was ₹2,299 in public facilities and ₹37,630 in private hospitals.
- **The JSSK sentence:** it reuses the verified week-29 tip.
- **The 270 days:** from the Ministry of WCD PMMVY FAQ: "A beneficiary is eligible to register in the PMMVY portal till 270
  days from child birth."

**A conflict to settle (Mohit or the doctor).** The verified PMMVY text says the first ₹3,000 comes "after at least one
antenatal check-up". So does the verified Learn article on schemes, which cites the ministry's FAQ. The FAQ says this in one
answer, but its conditions answer says "Registration of Pregnancy and at least two Ante-Natal Check-up (ANC) preferably within
six months from LMP".
- **What the app does now:** the new "apply now" line waits for two check-ups, which is true under either reading.
- **If two is right:** the verified PMMVY text and the article should change, for example: "₹3,000 after the pregnancy is
  registered and you have had two antenatal check-ups, best within six months of your last period".
- **If one is right:** the line can show after one.

- **"Find your nearest One Stop Centre (official)"**.

## 8. Version 31: fertile days, the chance of pregnancy, the countdown and the verdict (please check all of it)

Mohit asked for these on 23 Oct after feedback that another tracker's views help. Sakhi's earlier rule was never to predict
fertile days, so some doctor-checked lines that promised that had to change. They are listed first. Until this section is
checked and CDSCO decides the risk class, a release APK hides fertile days (`FERT_RELEASE` in `web/app.js`); the web prototype
and demo APKs show them.

**How the estimates are made** (please confirm the medicine, not just the words):
*Corrected in Version 32 (23 Oct, field-test audit 2.1): Version 31 used "the NHS gives 12 to 16 days" and 5 days for sperm.
The NHS page says ovulation comes "around 10 to 16 days before the next period" and that sperm can survive "for up to 7 days".
The rules below are the corrected ones.*
- **Ovulation:** most likely 14 days before her predicted next period. The NHS gives 10 to 16 days; our library article cites
  about 12 on average, from 7 to 17.
- **Fertile window:** the 6 days ending on ovulation (Wilcox 1995, cited in the library).
- **"Highest chance":** the 3 days ending on ovulation.
- **"Possible fertile days":** from 7 days before the earliest possible ovulation (16 days before her shortest gap) to the day
  after the latest (10 days before her longest), from her last 6 gaps.
- **Chance of pregnancy from sex without a method:** high or medium; on other days no chance is shown at all. Version 32 dropped
  the "low chance, not zero" label, which can read as a safe day. No day is ever called non-fertile or safe.
- **When it shows (Version 32):**
  - only while her stage is "Trying for a baby";
  - after 6 gaps of 21 to 35 days that vary by 7 days or less, at any age;
  - age 18 to 44;
  - not pregnant or in the year after a birth;
  - no hormones, and no method that stops ovulation or pregnancy;
  - never once her period is due or late.

**Doctor-checked lines that changed:**

| Where | Was | Now (English) | Now (Hindi) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Home and calendar note | This range is not a way to avoid pregnancy and shows no safe days. | These dates are estimates from your diary. They are not a way to avoid pregnancy. | ये तारीख़ें आपकी डायरी से अंदाज़ा हैं। ये गर्भ रोकने का तरीक़ा नहीं हैं। |
| Calendar note | Tap a day to log. No fertile or safe days are shown. | Tap a day to log. (With fertile days:) Fertile days are an estimate from your dates. There is no safe day: without a method, pregnancy can happen at any time of the month. | दर्ज करने के लिए दिन पर टैप करें। फ़र्टाइल दिन आपकी तारीख़ों से अंदाज़ा हैं। कोई दिन सुरक्षित नहीं: बिना तरीक़े के महीने में कभी भी गर्भ ठहर सकता है। |
| Contraception | Counting "safe days" is not a reliable way to avoid pregnancy, so this app never shows them. | Counting "safe days" is not a reliable way to avoid pregnancy, so this app never calls any day safe. Its fertile-day estimates are for planning a pregnancy. | “safe days” गिनना गर्भ से बचने का भरोसेमंद तरीक़ा नहीं है, इसलिए यह app किसी दिन को सुरक्षित नहीं कहता। इसके fertile दिनों के अंदाज़े गर्भ की योजना के लिए हैं। |
| Trying for a baby, temperature card | This app does not detect ovulation, marks no day, and shows no safe days. | This app does not detect ovulation: it only estimates the day from your dates, and it never shows safe days. | यह app ovulation नहीं पहचानता: सिर्फ़ आपकी तारीख़ों से दिन का अंदाज़ा लगाता है, और कोई safe days कभी नहीं दिखाता। |
| What your diary shows | Fertile days: shown only when your gaps stay within 7 days of each other. | Fertile days: an estimate, shown only after 6 gaps that stay within 7 days of each other. | fertile दिन: अंदाज़ा, तभी दिखते हैं जब 6 अंतर हों और वे आपस में 7 दिन के अंदर रहें। |
| Fertile days screen | Shown only when your cycles vary by 7 days or less. / …fewer than 3 logged gaps. | Shown only after 6 logged gaps that vary by 7 days or less. / …you are on hormones or a method, or there are fewer than 6 logged gaps. | (the same, in Hindi) |
| Library article "Why no app can name your fertile day", last paragraph | Two things this app will never do: name your fertile days, or name safe days. | Sakhi shows fertile days only as an estimate from your dates, for planning a pregnancy, and it never names safe days. Dates from any app are not a way to avoid pregnancy. | Sakhi 'फर्टाइल दिन' सिर्फ़ आपकी तारीख़ों से अंदाज़े के रूप में दिखाती है, प्रेग्नेंसी की योजना के लिए, और 'सेफ़ दिन' कभी नहीं बताती। … |

| Fertile screen | Highest chance · Possible fertile days · Fertile days are switched off in this version of Sakhi until doctors and the regulator approve them. | सबसे ज़्यादा संभावना · संभावित फ़र्टाइल दिन · Sakhi के इस संस्करण में फ़र्टाइल दिन बंद हैं, जब तक डॉक्टर और नियामक मंज़ूरी न दें। |

before the next period" · "Pads: {x}" · "Clots: {x}" · "Show more". All have Hindi in `web/app.js`.

**One wording choice to confirm with Mohit:** he asked for "non-fertile day" as the other tracker says it. Sakhi says "Low chance of
pregnancy, not zero" instead, because no date-based estimate can promise a day without risk. Changing it is one line.

**Superseded in Version 32:** Mohit's "non-fertile day" question is answered by decision 1 of the field-test audit. No label
shows on those days, and fertile days show only to a woman trying for a baby.

## 12. Version 35: the 12-week PMOS plan

**Approved:** Mohit, 23 Oct 2026: "plan approved build apk". `PLAN_RELEASE` is on, and the plan is in `0.35-pilot` (versionCode 40).
Wording added after this note needs its own review.

The plan is in `web/mods/plan.js`. It shows in demo builds and the web prototype only. Pilot, test and release builds show
it once `PLAN_RELEASE` in `web/app.js` is switched on after this review. The sources are in
`docs/lifestyle-plans-research-2026-10-23.md`, sections 2 and 8: the 2023 international PCOS guideline (recs 3.1 to 3.6),
ICMR-NIN Dietary Guidelines for Indians 2024, WHO 2020, the 2009 Indian obesity consensus, and the AYUSH Common Yoga Protocol.

### 12.1 Body: BMI and waist (optional, her own numbers)

| Where | English | Hindi |
|---|---|---|
| Range line | Healthy range for Indian women: 18.5 to 22.9 | भारतीय महिलाओं के लिए सही सीमा: 18.5 से 22.9 |
| **BMI under 18.5** | Your BMI is below the healthy range for Indian women. Eat regular meals with enough dal, milk, nuts and grains. If you are losing weight without trying, see a doctor. | आपका BMI भारतीय महिलाओं की सही सीमा से कम है। समय पर खाना खाएँ, जिसमें दाल, दूध, मेवे और अनाज भरपूर हों। अगर बिना कोशिश के वज़न घट रहा है, तो डॉक्टर को दिखाएँ। |
| BMI 18.5 to 22.9 | Your BMI is in the healthy range for Indian women. The plan keeps your weight steady and builds strength. | आपका BMI भारतीय महिलाओं की सही सीमा में है। प्लान आपका वज़न स्थिर रखता है और ताक़त बढ़ाता है। |
| **BMI 23 or more** | Your BMI is above the healthy range for Indian women. Small, steady changes help, and healthy habits help your body even before your weight changes. | आपका BMI भारतीय महिलाओं की सही सीमा से ज़्यादा है। छोटे, लगातार बदलाव मदद करते हैं, और अच्छी आदतें वज़न बदलने से पहले ही शरीर की मदद करती हैं। |
| **Waist 80 cm or more** | Your waist is 80 cm or more. In Indian women a bigger waist goes with a higher chance of sugar and heart problems. Aim to bring it below 80 cm, slowly. | आपकी कमर 80 सेमी या ज़्यादा है। भारतीय महिलाओं में बड़ी कमर के साथ शुगर और दिल की बीमारी का ख़तरा बढ़ता है। धीरे-धीरे इसे 80 सेमी से नीचे लाने का लक्ष्य रखें। |
| Waist under 80 cm | Your waist is under 80 cm, the limit for Indian women. | आपकी कमर 80 सेमी से कम है, जो भारतीय महिलाओं की सीमा है। |
| How to measure | Stand straight and breathe out. Put a tape round your waist halfway between your lowest rib and the top of your hip bone, usually near your navel. Snug, not tight. | सीधी खड़ी हों और साँस छोड़ें। … कसा हुआ, पर ज़्यादा टाइट नहीं। |
| Her focus | Habits first: "No weight goal. Habits help your cycles, skin and energy on their own." (the default) · Habits and weight: "A little more activity each week, up to 250 minutes. Your weight shows in your progress." | पहले आदतें · आदतें और वज़न |
| Footnote | BMI is a rough guide: build, muscle and age matter too. The limits for Indian women come from the 2009 national consensus: BMI 23 and waist 80 cm. | … |

No "obese" or "overweight" label appears anywhere. No weight target, no calorie counting, and no weight prediction.

### 12.2 The weekly target (numbers, please check)

- **Active minutes a week** build up from 50 ("hardly any exercise"), 75 ("some days") or 120 ("most days") to 150 by week 3 to
  11.
- With "habits and weight" the target goes on to 250, as the guideline advises for modest weight loss. It stays at 150 if her
  BMI is already under 23.
- **Today's suggestion** is a fifth of the week's minutes, at least 10. There are 2 strength days a week, not one after the
  other; a beginner has 1 in week 1.
- **Walking:** "Brisk walking is the easiest way to add minutes: fast enough that you can talk, but not sing."
- **Sun salutation:** offered from week 3 (week 5 for a beginner), never with back pain. 3 rounds, rising to 6 by week 10, one
  slow breath (6 seconds) in each pose.

### 12.3 Food (please check the avoid list and the six habits)

- **Plate:** half sabzi and salad, a quarter roti, rice or millet, a quarter dal, chana, paneer, egg or fish, and curd or chaas
  on the side. "No food is banned. Eat the green ones more often and the yellow ones less often."
- **Avoid:** Vanaspati and dalda (trans fat) · Crash diets, or eating very little · Weight-loss pills or powders without a
  doctor. Also: "No diet pills, weight-loss powders or supplements without your doctor."
- **Less often:** maida (white bread, biscuits, naan) · big helpings of white rice · sugar, mithai, sweets made with gur · cold
  drinks and packaged juice · fried snacks (namkeen, samosa, pakora) · packet food (chips, instant noodles) · extra salt (pickle,
  papad, sauces).
- **Six habits, one more every two weeks:**
  1. Half the plate sabzi
  2. Protein at breakfast (dal, besan cheela, sprouts, paneer, curd or an egg)
  3. One millet or whole-grain swap a day
  4. No sweet drinks
  5. A better snack (fruit, roasted chana or peanuts instead of biscuits, namkeen or mithai)
  6. Less oil
- **Regional lists:** six regions, from her PIN code or her choice (North, West, Central, South, East, North-east). They list
  everyday local grains, dals, greens, fruit, drinks and snacks, for example:
  - West: bajra or jowar bhakri, nachni bhakri, matki usal, ker-sangri, chaas;
  - South: ragi mudde, millet upma, sambar with more dal, keerai, buttermilk.

  Please read the lists in `LOCAL` in `web/mods/plan.js`. No folk bans or Ayurvedic food rules are included.

### 12.4 Yoga and moves (please check, ideally with a physiotherapist)

- **Five routines:**
  - Gentle start: loosen up, march, tadasana, vrikshasana, trikonasana, bhadrasana, vakrasana, setu bandhasana,
    pavanamuktasana, shavasana.
  - Cycle care: loosen up, shashankasana, bhadrasana, ardha ushtrasana, bhujangasana, ardha shalabhasana, setu bandhasana,
    pavanamuktasana, shavasana.
  - Sun salutation, slow.
  - Strength at home: chair squats, wall push-ups, step-ups, bridge lifts and heel raises, 10 to 15 times, 2 sets.
  - Calm breath: anulom vilom without holding the breath, bhramari, sitting quietly.

  Each move has its own one-line "how" in `R` in `plan.js`.
- **Knee pain:** child pose becomes a seated forward bend on a chair, half camel becomes tadasana, and step-ups become
  marching.
- **Back pain:** no half locust and no sun salutation.
- **Every routine:** "Stop and rest if you feel pain, dizziness, chest pain, breathlessness that doesn’t settle, or bleeding.
  During your period, do what feels comfortable and skip what hurts."
- **Sun salutation:** "Skip this if you have back pain or high BP, or feel dizzy. Do Gentle start instead."
- **Not offered:**
  - In pregnancy: "Your plan is paused while you are pregnant…"
  - For 6 weeks after a birth: "Start this plan after your 6-week check. Until then, short walks and the pelvic floor course
    are best."

### 12.5 Progress and what to expect (please check the timeline)

| Where | English | Hindi |
|---|---|---|

- Strength: 1.3 days a week
- Food habits kept: 4 of the last 7 days

## 13. Version 36: plans suggested from her diary, and the joining question (please check)

Built on 23 Oct 2026 and not shipped yet. Mohit asked for plans suggested "based on symptomatic conditions". The full design,
with the points behind every suggestion, is in `docs/plans-audit-2026-10-23.md` (sections 3 and 4). Please check two
things: **which plan Sakhi suggests for which sign** (13.1), and **the sentences** (13.2 to 13.4).

### 13.1 Which plan for which sign (please check)

| She logged or said | Sakhi suggests first |
|---|---|
| She has PMOS (her own answer), or her self-check matched PMOS | My PMOS plan (12 weeks) |
| 2 or more cycles over 35 days, PMOS signs in check-ins, no diagnosis | "Could it be PMOS?" (the self-check). **Never the PMOS plan with "you have PMOS".** |
| Heavy flow (soaked a pad in 1 to 2 hours, clots as big as a ₹10 coin, leaks), or tired on 5+ days a month | More iron in 21 days (iron-rich food) |
| Periods that needed a painkiller, or cramps logged | For period cramps (3-minute routine) |
| Low mood, anxiety or irritability | When you feel anxious or low (3 minutes); Mood after birth after a birth |

| Hot flushes (40+) | For hot flushes (3 minutes), then the weekly symptom diary |
| Pregnant | My pregnancy plan, iron tablets, food in pregnancy; nausea: the nausea routine |
| After a birth | Daily recovery check (first 6 weeks), pelvic floor trainer, breastfeeding |
| Trying | Before you start: checklist |
| Bones and strength (40+) | Bones and heart, Strength at home |

In pregnancy Sakhi never suggests the PMOS plan or the cramps routine. In the first 6 weeks after a birth the PMOS plan and the
moves wait for her check-up.

## 14. Version 37: Sakhi as her companion (please check 14.1 to 14.6)

Built on 23 and 24 Oct 2026 and not given to testers yet. Mohit asked that Sakhi understand a woman's needs when she joins,
then guide her for 12 weeks and push her to note what she did and didn't do. The design and the research behind it are in
`docs/companion-journey-2026-10-23.md`.

How it works:
- Joining asks one goal, then only the questions her plan needs (14.1, 14.2).
- She then sees her plan: her goal, where she is now in her own answers, her 12 weeks, what to expect, and a doctor check
  when her answers need one (14.3, 14.4).
- Each day Today shows 2 or 3 steps (14.5). In the evening she says what she did, and Sakhi says what each habit does for her
  (14.6).
- My plan shows her 12 weeks, a look-back each month, her results and her care team (14.7).

What we kept to:
- No promised result or percentage anywhere (Drugs and Magic Remedies Act, section 3(c)). The wording says what a habit does
  and that "it is different for every woman".
- No supplements, herbs or medicines.
- The doctor checks and red flags come before any habit.

**Questions for you:**
1. **14.4:** the doctor check shows when months get skipped, or when periods come more than 45 days apart or change a lot.
   Should periods 36 to 45 days apart, with no skipped months, get it too?
2. **14.4:** the heavy-bleeding check shows for a pad every 1 to 2 hours (or two at once), or clots as big as a ₹10 coin.
   Should "often tired, dizzy or out of breath" on its own also bring the Hb test?
3. **14.6:** is each "what it does for you" line true as written, and soft enough? We changed "keeps bones strong" to "helps
   keep bones strong". Please flag any other line that reads like a promise.
4. **14.5:** the bedtime step moves her bedtime earlier in steps of 30 minutes, every 4 weeks, to 11 pm at most. A woman who
   sleeps after midnight starts at midnight. After night shifts the step is "Sleep 7 to 8 hours after your shift". Is that
   right?
5. **14.2:** the signs she can tick (extra hair, acne, hair thinning, dark velvety skin on the neck) are the PMOS
   self-check's own. In joining they only show on the reveal, as "where she is now". They don't diagnose, and they don't
   change the plan. Is that the right use?

| Small food changes add up: steadier sugar levels help your hormones, and your cycle, over time. | खाने के छोटे बदलाव मिलकर असर करते हैं: शुगर स्थिर रहने से समय के साथ हॉर्मोन और पीरियड, दोनों को मदद मिलती है। |
| A regular bedtime helps your body clock, and your body clock and your cycle are linked. | सोने का तय समय शरीर की घड़ी को ठीक रखता है, और शरीर की घड़ी और पीरियड आपस में जुड़े हैं। |

| Regular movement makes period pain milder for many women. | नियमित चलने-फिरने से कई महिलाओं में पीरियड का दर्द कम होता है। |

## 15. Versions 37.1 and 37.2: her first day, and a companion that notices (please check 15.1, 15.2 and 15.4)

Built on 24 Oct 2026 and not given to testers yet. Mohit joined 0.37-pilot on his own phone and felt he went "straight on
to program". Now her plan is shown as a few short cards, and it starts only when she taps "Start my plan" (today, tomorrow
or Monday, with her evening check-in time). Today keeps her first 3 days for her plan. The design is in
`docs/first-days-audit-2026-10-24.md`.

What we kept to, as in section 14:
- No promised result or percentage.
- No supplements, herbs or medicines.
- The doctor checks and red flags come before any habit.

Her steps and what each does for her reuse the lines from 14.3 and 14.6 word for word; only the lines below are new.

**Questions for you:**
1. **15.1:** "Periods often take 3 to 6 months to change." It shows on all four period plans: irregular periods, painful
   periods, heavy periods and mood before periods. Is it right for all four, or only for irregular periods?
2. **15.2:** on her plan's first 3 days, the "keep an eye on it" cards wait. Examples: a period late by less than 90 days,
   spotting between periods once or twice, one short or long gap. These still show at once: a gap of 90 days or more,
   severe pain, low mood after a birth, and everything urgent. Is a 3-day wait safe for the first group?
3. **15.1:** the doctor-check card. For skipped months it says "Get the check in the next 3 weeks; Sakhi will ask you about
   it." For heavy bleeding or pain that stops her day it says "Please see a doctor soon." Is each one urgent enough?
4. **15.2:** on day 3, once: "Questions about your plan? A Qura doctor can go through it with you. The first 20-minute call
   is free." Is that all right?

### 15.4 Version 37.2: a companion that notices (please check)

Built on 24 Oct 2026 after 15.1 to 15.3, also not given to testers. Sakhi now:
- notices her period during the plan, and says so that day;
- moves the week's look-back onto Today (her energy only; what got in the way comes from her check-ins);
- ends the 12 weeks with an honest count and her results;
- folds "no period yet" into the plan's own doctor check while that card shows (before 90 days);
- uses midlife words for "no period yet" from 40.

The design is in `docs/ux-gaps-2026-10-24.md`.

**Questions for you:**
5. **Her period:** "Your period came after {n} days. Sakhi noted it for your results." On the period-pain plan it adds "Your
   cramps routine is on Today on your period days." Is that all right?
6. **From 40:** "Day {n} since your last period. In the years before menopause, gaps between periods often get longer or
   shorter. Keep noting your periods." It replaces "Your period is later than the usual 21 to 35 days". The 90-day "see a
   doctor" line stays as it is. Is that safe?
7. **The end of 12 weeks:** if her periods are still far apart (or didn't come), every period was still painful, or still
   heavy, it says "If nothing has changed, see a doctor: they can look for the cause." Is that the right moment and wording?
8. **The doctor check on Today** now repeats the reveal's line: "If your period is late and you could be pregnant, do a
   pregnancy test first." Good?

## 16. Version 38: a plan that fits every goal (please check 16.1)

Built on 24 Oct 2026 and not given to testers yet. The design is in `docs/ux-gaps-2026-10-24.md`.
- **One pregnancy plan:** what her pregnancy plan has due now is her plan's first step. Her weeks of pregnancy and her
  trimesters replace "12 weeks".
- **After a birth:** every goal starts with the daily recovery check (its danger signs unchanged) until 6 weeks, done only
  by doing the check.
- **Trying for a baby:** its own plan (a walk, the food habit, and the before-pregnancy checklist once a week, as a link
  with no dose). Its expectation is the trying page's own reviewed line: more than 8 in 10 couples within a year.
- **Heavy periods:** the iron steps follow what she eats, then one iron food a day after day 21.
- **From 40:** Today shows days or months since her last period and her weekly diary, not a cycle countdown.
- **Mood before periods:** a "calm week" line in the 7 days before her likely period.
- **One name each:** the monthly signs check-in is "This month", and the PMOS self-check opens with her plan's answers
  (she still answers the rest before any verdict).

**Questions for you:**
1. **16.1, iron:** is "Poha has iron, and lemon helps your body take it in" right? Is the vegetarian list (green leafy
   sabzi, chana, rajma, ragi or poha, with lemon or amla) good for a daily habit after the first 21 days?
2. **16.1, from 40:** "Around 40 and after, gaps between periods change. Keep noting each period." Is that right on Today,
   where the cycle countdown was?
3. **16.1, the calm week:** "Your calm week: the days before your period. Your calm breath comes first." Is it all right
   for mood and body changes before periods?
4. **16.1, before a pregnancy:** "Eating well before a pregnancy helps your body get ready" and "The checklist covers what
   doctors advise before a pregnancy." Is that all right?

## 18. Version 39: the three pillars' journey (please check 18.0 first)

Version 39 builds the merged roadmap (`docs/roadmap-v39-2026-10-24.md`). Most of it reuses lines already reviewed; this
section holds the decisions and the few new lines that touch health. Section 19 holds interface labels (please glance).

### 18.0 Version 39.0: three decisions for the gynaecologist (please check)

These change when a reviewed line shows, not its words. Until you decide, the app shows the stricter reviewed line.

| # | What the app now does | Reviewed line it shows | Please confirm |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | At 45 or older (or in midlife), bleeding logged 365 days or more after the one before shows "Bleeding after your periods stopped · See a doctor this week", even if she never ticked "periods stopped". After 12 months with no period, the menopause timeline asks once "Have your periods stopped?" | explain.js `bleed_after_stop`: "Any bleeding after your periods have stopped needs a check, even a little." / "See a doctor within 2 weeks." | Is 365 days the right trigger? Is "this week" on Today (the card's level) right, against "within 2 weeks" in its own text? |
| 2 | Pregnancy logs: a BP of 160/110 or more (or high BP with symptoms) in the last 3 days shows on Today as "Call 112 now"; 140/90 or more as "See a doctor today"; Hb under 7 as "See a doctor today"; a 75 g 2-hour sugar of 140 or more, or a fasting sugar of 95 or more, as "See a doctor this week" | preg.js `bp_urgent`, `bp_today`, `hb_flag`, `sug_flag`, `sug_fflag`, `sug_fflag2` | Is this mapping of log flags to urgency levels right? |
| 3 | Where two reviewed lines disagree in pregnancy, the stricter one now shows: bleeding in pregnancy and "Baby moving less" (a new day-sheet chip from 28 weeks) are "Call 112 now", led by Help's line | Help `s1c` ("In pregnancy: bleeding, fits, severe headache with blurred vision, fluid leaking, baby moving less"), with pregtools `k_today` as the explanation for movements | Bleeding in pregnancy: "call 112 now" for any bleeding, or "see a doctor today" for light spotting (explain.js `p_bleed` said today)? |

Also from 39.0, with no new words:
- After a birth, today's and yesterday's recovery-check warning signs show on Today at their own level, in the check's own words
  (post.js `fl_*`).
- Headache and swelling in pregnancy now count when they are logged on different days within 3 days of each other.
- The plan's "kick counter" step is gone (no kick counting to a number).
- The supplements card ("Want advice on supplements or medicines?") is gone from My progress and the end of a plan.
- Hindi: "energy" in the weekly look-back is ऊर्जा (it was ताक़त, which means strength).

### 18.0b Version 39.0: changed after the 24 Oct review (please check)

A review by 3 agents (each finding retried by a skeptic) confirmed 34 findings; all are fixed. These touch when a reviewed line
shows:
- **"Have your periods stopped?" → "Yes"** now dates the stop to her last period, not the day she answers, so bleeding logged
  after her last period (including that day) keeps "Bleeding after your periods stopped". The "My periods have stopped" stage
  does the same.
- **The 365-day rule (18.0 #1)** no longer applies on a hormonal method (or after stopping one within that year), while
  breastfeeding, or to the first bleed after a pregnancy or a birth.
- **A monthly check-in's "bleeding in pregnancy" tick** is "Call 112 now" only for 2 days. After that it shows "See a doctor
  today" (its own reviewed line), for as long as the check-in counts (45 days).
- **After a birth:** Today's card leads with the recovery check's own lead line: "Call 112 now, or go to the nearest
  hospital" (urgent signs) or "See a doctor today". For thoughts of harming herself or her baby, Today's card and the shared
  phone's card call **Tele-MANAS 14416** first, then 112. A recovery check today replaces yesterday's.
- **"Not yet"** keeps the 365-day card for a later bleed. The second agent judged this the documented rule; please say if her
  "Not yet" should switch it off.

### 18.1 Version 39.1: when existing reviewed cards show (please check)

No new health wording. These change when What your diary shows uses its own reviewed cards, so Diary's "What it shows" and
its verdict never disagree:

| # | What the app now does | Reviewed card | Please confirm |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Trends says "Outside the usual" because of an older gap (one of her last 6): that gap gets the long or short gap card, titled "One of your recent gaps was {n} days". It stays off Today unless 2 or more of her last 6 gaps are outside the range. | `gap_long`, `gap_short` | Fine as "Keep an eye on it", or "See a doctor this month" at 2 or more? |
| 2 | Trends says "Irregular" (every gap 21 to 35 days, but they vary by more than 7 days, or 9 under 26 and over 41): a card "Your gaps vary by {n} days" with the verdict's own line ("Usual: 21 to {b} days apart, varying by {v} days or less."), "Keep an eye on it", off Today | Trends `vd_cyc_usual` | Is "Keep an eye on it" right? |
| 3 | A period longer than 8 days in the last 6 months (as Trends counts it) gets the long-period card ("See a doctor this week"), titled "One of your recent periods lasted {n} days" when it isn't her latest. Older than 75 days, it stays off Today. | `period_long` | Is "this week" right for one that is months old? |
| 4 | Pain that stopped her day in 2 of her last 3 periods shows the severe-pain card, even when the latest was easier | `pain_severe` | Is 2 of 3 right? |
| 5 | Her heavy-bleeding chart (PBAC) with a total of 100 or more shows the heavy-flow card ("See a doctor this week") | `flow_heavy`; cond.js's own line "Doctors use a total of 100 or more as a sign of heavy periods" | Is 100 the right line for the card? |
| 6 | Flow and pain can be added on any day of her period, and on the day it ends; the heavy-flow and severe-pain cards, her report and the self-checks read them all | `flow_heavy`, `pain_severe` | (no question) |
| 7 | Each long-gap, PMOS-signs, heavy-flow, long-period and severe-pain card offers "Check the signs (2 minutes)": the PMOS, fibroids or endometriosis self-check, until she has done it in the last 90 days. Pilot and demo builds only. | selfcheck.js | Are these the right checks for each card? |

Pattern check is gone. Its two questions moved into the PMOS self-check, in their reviewed words: "Has a doctor already told
you that you have PMOS?" (her PMOS line, as on Me) and, after the 9 signs and not counted with them, "Any milky discharge from
the nipples?" with its reviewed answer about a prolactin blood test.

| My plan ({t}): questions / My plan ({t}): results (the call form's topic) | मेरा प्लान ({t}): सवाल / मेरा प्लान ({t}): नतीजे |

### 18.3 Version 39.7: knowledge that leads somewhere (please check)

**1. One danger list in pregnancy (the crosswalk).** The pregnancy signs that mean "go now" were written six ways. Version 39.7
uses one list, the reviewed line from "Your four check-ups": *bleeding, leaking water, fever, severe headache or blurred vision,
fits, swelling of face or hands, or the baby moving less after 28 weeks* (ब्लीडिंग, पानी निकलना, बुखार, तेज़ सिरदर्द या धुंधला दिखना,
झटके, चेहरे या हाथों पर सूजन या 28 हफ़्ते के बाद बच्चा कम हिले). Where two reviewed lines disagreed, the stricter now shows (your
decision 2, until you decide).

| Sign | Learn guide "Signs that mean go now" | "Your four check-ups" | Help (Call 112 now) | Family cards | Husband's page | Symptom guide | Now shown everywhere |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Bleeding | Go now | Go straight in | Now | At once | 112 or 102 now | Now | Now |
| Leaking water | Go now | Go straight in | Now | At once | Now | Now | Now |
| Fever | Go now | Go straight in | *not listed* | At once | *not listed* | **Today** | **Now** (the symptom guide's "Fever, or burning when you pee" is now "Burning when you pee", Today) |
| Severe headache or blurred vision | Go now | Go straight in | "with" blurred vision | "or" | "with" | Today ("a headache that will not go away") | Now ("or"); the lasting headache stays Today |
| Fits | Go now | Go straight in | Now | At once | Now | Now | Now |
| Swelling of face or hands | Go now | Go straight in | *not listed* | At once (face) | *not listed* | **Today** | **Now** (the symptom guide's swelling line is now only the headache, Today) |
| Baby moving less after 28 weeks | Go now | Go straight in | Now | At once (separate line) | Now | Now | Now |

Please confirm the list, and these two that stay as they were but the Learn guide puts at "go now": itching all over in the
last months, and very little urine or vomiting that won't stop (the symptom guide says Today for both; Version 40's safety bank
settles them). Other languages (Marathi, Bengali, Tamil, Telugu) still show the old 5-sign Help line until it is translated.

**2. New lines (pilot builds; please check):**

| # | Where | English | Hindi |

If there is bleeding, pain or leaking water, stop and see a doctor. | ठीक चल रहे गर्भ में संबंध बनाना सुरक्षित है, जब तक डॉक्टर ने मना न किया हो, जैसे जब आँवल (placenta) नीचे हो। ब्लीडिंग, दर्द या पानी निकले तो रुकें और डॉक्टर को दिखाएँ। |

If your weight worries you, a doctor can help you pick another method. | भ्रम: गर्भनिरोधक गोलियों से ज़्यादातर औरतों का वज़न बढ़ जाता है। / शोध में यह नहीं पाया गया कि गोली से औरतों का वज़न बढ़ता है। … |

| 8 | Same (from the reviewed method pages) | Myth: The Antara injection makes a woman unable to have children. / Myth: NSV, the operation for men, makes a man weak. (answers: the reviewed Antara and NSV lines) | भ्रम: अंतरा इंजेक्शन से औरत को बच्चा नहीं हो पाता। / भ्रम: NSV, पुरुषों का ऑपरेशन, आदमी को कमज़ोर बना देता है। |

Sources for the new lines: NHS 111 Wales and nidirect (sex in pregnancy); NHS, Travelling in pregnancy; NHS, Your body after the
birth; WHO Family Planning Handbook (an IUD does not move to the heart or brain; the pill does not cause infertility); the
Cochrane review of combined contraceptives and weight (no causal link found).

**3. Built only from reviewed lines (please glance):** the periods dos and don'ts guide, the parts of the pregnancy and
after-birth guides not in the table, and one weekly "Did you know?" card on Today (a Sach ya Myth card). The periods myths page
is now a view of the Sach ya Myth deck: its 6 own myths joined the deck in its reviewed words, and the period-blood card took the
page's fuller answer ("A period that stops is not 'bad blood stuck inside'").

**4. One number for you to pick: changing a pad.** The app says "every 4 to 6 hours" (Sach ya Myth, the family card, the
periods dos and don'ts), "4–8 hours" (the girls' first-period page and Learn guide), "at least every 6 hours" (the pads Learn
guide) and "every few hours" (the period products page). Please pick one; every copy then changes together.

**5. Corrected at the official source (24 Oct; please glance):**
- PMMVY's first part needs the pregnancy registered and one check-up within 6 months of the last period (Mission Shakti
  guidelines, 2022; PIB, Aug 2025). The portal's FAQ still says two, so the "apply now" line adds "Some offices still ask for two
  check-ups; your Anganwadi worker will know." A second child who is a girl counts only if the pregnancy was registered.
  PM-KISAN counts for a woman farmer in her own name. Odisha now pays PMMVY with MAMATA (MAMATA-PMMVY); Telangana doesn't run PMMVY
  yet (its MCH Kit, earlier KCR Kit).
- Kilkari runs only in some states, and calls the number given at registration (Lok Sabha, 9 Feb 2024).
- The Supreme Court ordered free pads in schools on 30 Jan 2026, and on 1 Sep 2026 said they must be biodegradable and compostable.
  Some states now give the ₹6 Menstrual Hygiene Scheme pads free (Health Ministry letter, April 2026).
- Paid leave after adopting: 12 weeks for a child of any age, from the day the child is handed over (Supreme Court, 17 Mar 2026).
- Every helpline number the app shows was confirmed, except the CARA adoption line 1800-11-1311 (CARA's site would not open; a
  West Bengal government page lists it).

### 18.3a After the review of V39.7 (corrections; please glance)

**Corrected to match the official-source check (MATERNITY-1, MATERNITY-2):**
- The Learn guide "What the government gives you, free, and who to ask", PMMVY:
  - **EN:** Rs 5,000 for your first child, in two parts: Rs 3,000 once your pregnancy is registered and you have had at
    least one antenatal check-up within 6 months of your last period, and Rs 2,000 after the birth is registered and the
    baby has had the vaccines due by 14 weeks. Rs 6,000 in one go if your second child is a girl; for this, register during
    the pregnancy. You need one proof, such as SC/ST, 40% disability, a BPL or NFSA ration card, an Ayushman (PM-JAY) card,
    family income under Rs 8 lakh a year, an MGNREGA job card, PM-KISAN in your own name as a woman farmer, or an e-Shram
    card. Register with your Anganwadi worker or ASHA, or on the PMMVY portal, up to 270 days after the birth. Not for women
    in regular government jobs, or women who already get paid maternity benefit under another law.
  - **HI:** the same, in the card's reviewed Hindi.

**New on the PMMVY claim (labels and one line from the reviewed card):**

|---|---|
| First or second child? / First child / Second child | पहला या दूसरा बच्चा? / पहला बच्चा / दूसरा बच्चा |
| For a second child, register during the pregnancy. If the baby is a girl, ₹6,000 comes in one payment after the birth and the 14-week vaccines. | दूसरे बच्चे के लिए गर्भ के दौरान ही पंजीकरण कराएँ। बेटी हो तो जन्म और 14 हफ़्ते के टीकों के बाद ₹6,000 एक बार में मिलते हैं। |

  देते हैं।"

### 18.3c After the review of V39.9: which guide answers which question (please glance)

No new advice: these rules choose which reviewed guide comes first when she searches.
- **After a loss or an abortion** ("garbhpat ke baad kya kare", "गर्भ गिर गया क्या करें", "pregnancy loss ke baad"): the
  guide "After a pregnancy loss: care and support". It was the pregnancy dos and don'ts.
- **Pregnant, and a loss happening now** ("garbhpat ho raha hai kya kare"): "Signs that mean go now, not tomorrow".
- **Asking for an abortion** ("abortion karana hai"), contraception ("गर्भनिरोधक गोली भूल गई", "pregnancy rokne") or
  how to get pregnant: no guide first; the tools lead (the abortion, missed-pill and trying pages).
- **Pregnant, "period jaisa dard"** (pain like a period): the pregnancy dos and don'ts, whose go-now signs cover pain. It
  was the periods guide.
- **After a birth, "pregnancy ke baad kya kare"**: the after-birth dos and don'ts.
- **Pregnant, the danger guide now also comes first for** a severe headache ("severe headache", "sir me tez dard") and the
  baby moving less ("bachcha kam hil raha hai", "हलचल कम"), 2 of Help's 7 signs. The kick counter is the first tool for
  movements.
- **Pregnant, the danger guide no longer comes first for** "khoon ki kami" (anaemia), "blood test", "blood group",
  "blood sugar" or "BP". "Khoon aa raha hai" (bleeding) still brings it.

- 40+ bones: Milk · Dahi · Ragi · Til · Paneer · Green leafy sabzi · Rajma · Chana · Small fish (milk foods only for women who
  take milk).
- Fasting: Roti · Curd · Dalia · Poha · Eggs · Paneer · Chaas · Cucumber, tomato · Coconut water · Dates · ORS.
- Pregnancy food: Roti · Rice · Ragi · Dal · Rajma · Milk · Curd · Vegetables · Green leafy · Guava · Banana · Peanuts.

**The picture check (pilot builds, for the field-test coordinator only, never shown to women as text):** each drawing's name,
such as Bleeding, Water leaking, Fever, A bad headache, Fits, Swelling, The baby moving less, Hard to breathe, Dizzy or
fainting, Belly pain and Call for help, then the 42 food and kitchen drawings. The coordinator asks "What is this?" and notes
whether she names it. The roadmap's bar is 85% for each drawing.

### 19.28 Version 39.28: your doctor first (MODULE_API section 71)

Mohit, 28 Oct: most women in tier 1 and 2 cities see a private gynaecologist, so Sakhi shouldn't always send them to the ASHA
or ANM. One question while she joins sets where Sakhi sends her. A government centre or her ASHA keeps today's reviewed
words. Before she answers, and with a private doctor or "Nowhere yet", the lines below take their place. The doses, the
tests and their weeks, the danger signs and the emergency numbers don't change.

**Reused, already reviewed:** every dose, test and week in these lines (iron 180 days, folic acid 400 mcg to the end of the
third month, the 75 g sugar test, Td's two doses or one booster, the 18–20 week anomaly scan, the 6-week check's list, the
vaccine schedule), and "Many mothers skip it. Please go."

**Please review.** Questions for the doctor:
1. **"At least 4 check-ups."** The pregnancy plan keeps its 4 check-ups and says: "You need at least 4 check-ups; your
   gynaecologist may see you more often." Is "at least 4" right to show, given India's minimum of 4 and the WHO's 8 contacts?
2. **Iron as her doctor prescribed.** Her doctor's dose offers "Every day", "Every other day" or "Once a week". Every other day
   rings 2 days after her last tablet, and a tablet yesterday makes today a day off. Fine to offer these three?
3. **Who to ask in the iron help lines.** "Ask your doctor for a haemoglobin test this week. Below 7 needs care in a
   hospital." (before: "ask the ANM… Below 7 needs a higher centre") and "…no rise in haemoglobin after 4 weeks of tablets:
   tell your doctor." (before: "ask the ANM to refer you"). Fine?
4. **Iron and calcium in pregnancy and after the birth** now say "the iron tablet your doctor prescribed" and "the calcium your
   doctor prescribed", without the national schedule's doses (one red tablet a day; calcium with lunch and dinner). The
   timing advice stays. Is leaving the dose to her doctor right for a woman with a private gynaecologist?
5. **The quiet lines.** Free government care becomes one line each: the free iron tablet, "Free at government hospitals:
   check-ups on the 9th (PMSMA), the birth (JSSK) and scheme money (PMMVY)", and "Vaccines in the national schedule are free
   at government centres". Enough?

### 19.29 Version 39.29: the doctor route everywhere (MODULE_API section 72)

Mohit, 28 Oct: carry Version 39.28's doctor-first rule into the Learn library and the other modules. A woman who sees a private
doctor (or hasn't said) reads 219 lines in their doctor-route form: 73 Learn paragraphs, 69 strings and 77 lines written in place.
Each is the reviewed line with only who she sees, or where she gets it, changed. The doctor first, the free government option kept
as a second clause. The full list, in English and Hindi beside the reviewed English: **`docs/doctor-route-review-2026-10-28.md`**.

**Unchanged for everyone (reviewed):** emergency numbers and danger signs, and where to go in an emergency. Also:
- every dose, test and week;
- the government services pages, My ASHA and the Learn services guides;
- scheme facts, the free-supply and free-test lists, the HPV vaccine programme and the teen (RKSK) pages.

**Please review.** Questions for the doctor:
1. **Who to ask when unsure in pregnancy.** "Not sure if it counts: call your doctor, or 104" (before: "call your ANM"). The
   danger signs, 112/108/102 and "a CHC, FRU or district hospital is best" stay as they are. Right to leave the emergency
   destination unchanged for a woman with a private gynaecologist?
2. **High blood pressure or Hb below 7 in pregnancy.** Before: "the ANM should refer you to a higher centre". Now: "this needs
   your gynaecologist's care now, in a hospital if they say so". Strong enough?
3. **The emergency pill.** "Any chemist sells it without a prescription; it is also free from your ASHA, the sub-centre, PHC or
   CHC." And for missed pills, "ask your doctor whether you need the emergency pill" (before: "your ASHA or doctor"). Fine?
4. **After the birth.** "If an ASHA visits you, she comes on days 3, 7, 14, 21, 28 and 42", and "Keep taking the iron and
   calcium your doctor prescribed, for as long as they said" (before: "for 180 days … six months"). Is leaving the length to her
   doctor right here, as on the check-ups page (question 4 of 19.28)?
5. **Screening.** "From 30, every woman can have a cervical screening and a breast exam once in 5 years, with her gynaecologist
   or free at the Ayushman Arogya Mandir or PHC." The intervals are unchanged. Fine?
6. **The iron guide's title.** "The red tablet, the white tablet and two injections" names the government's tablets. For a woman
   on her doctor's brands, keep the title, or give her a plainer one ("Iron, calcium and two injections")?

### 19.30 Version 39.30: a danger sign typed in search, for every woman (MODULE_API section 73)

No new wording. Help's own lines, already reviewed, now also appear above the search results when a woman types one of Help's
"Call 112 now" signs in her own words, at every stage (before, only in pregnancy did search put the danger guide first):
- "Call 112 now if" with "Soaking a pad every 1–2 hours and feeling dizzy or breathless" (bleeding with dizziness, fainting or
  breathlessness), or with "Period late or a positive test, plus one-sided lower belly pain, shoulder-tip pain, dizziness or
  fainting"; buttons "Call 112 now" and "Help and safety";
- "Thoughts of harming myself or my baby" with "Tele-MANAS 14416 and 112: free, any time"; buttons Call 14416, Call 112 and
  "Help and safety".

One question for the doctor: these two signs show a card only together with dizziness, fainting or breathlessness (or, for a
late period, one-sided or shoulder pain). Bleeding alone, or dizziness alone, shows the normal results. Is that the right line,
or should heavy bleeding alone ("a pad every 1–2 hours") show Help's "See a doctor today if" card as well?

### 19.32 Version 39.32: Getting ready for a baby, the full plan (MODULE_API section 75)

Built on 28 Oct 2026 (Mohit: "Build this plan personalised to users who are planning for a baby"). Every line of the plan is in
its own packet, as built: `docs/baby-plan-review-packet-2026-10-28.md`, also a page where the doctor can reply line by line
(claude.ai/artifact/XXz1VPJicgVJMAY6v3rrUt; Mohit shares it). Signing the packet signs this section.

- **New, please check:** 101 lines (about 1,270 English words), each with its Hindi: the start questions, the doctor cards
  for each version, the tablet's page and step, the 7 new food habits and their reasons, the lines on Yoga and moves, the 12
  weekly and 4 monthly lessons, the 1-minute ready check, My plan's 12 weeks, her doctor month, a positive test, a fresh start
  and her results.
- **How it works, please check:** 21 rules (her version, the 4th step, the food sets, the moves before her period, the ready

  और हफ़्ते में लगभग {m} मिनट चलना-फिरना।" (a shortened copy of section 19.32's line, which waits for the doctor).

#### 19.33.1 The recommended wordings (Mohit, 29 Oct: "use recommended wordings")

These are health lines, so they wait for the doctor. Mohit chose to use them in the pilot meanwhile, as every Version 39 line has been.

    दिशानिर्देशों से रोज़ की आदतें, और गर्भ से पहले की folic acid की गोली, जिसकी वे सलाह देते हैं। आपके जवाबों से आपके प्लान का
    रूप चुना जाता है। यहाँ और कोई सप्लीमेंट, जड़ी-बूटी या दवा नहीं: वह आपकी डॉक्टर तय करती हैं।"
  - The pregnancy plan (it has an iron tablet step, so the same contradiction): "Everyday habits from national guidelines, and
    the iron tablets your doctor prescribes in pregnancy. No other supplements, herbs or medicines here: your doctor decides
    those." / "राष्ट्रीय दिशानिर्देशों से रोज़ की आदतें, और गर्भ में आपकी डॉक्टर की लिखी आयरन की गोलियाँ। यहाँ और कोई सप्लीमेंट,
    जड़ी-बूटी या दवा नहीं: वह आपकी डॉक्टर तय करती हैं।"
  - Either, on a shared phone: "Everyday habits from national guidelines. Any other tablet, herb or medicine: your doctor
    decides." / "राष्ट्रीय दिशानिर्देशों से रोज़ की आदतें। कोई और गोली, जड़ी-बूटी या दवा: वह आपकी डॉक्टर तय करती हैं।"
  - The other plans keep "the same for every woman. No supplements, herbs or medicines here".
- **What consistency brings, Getting ready** (gap 8: it showed only the conception figure, which keeping to the plan doesn't
  bring). Three labelled lines, the first two being their steps' reviewed reasons and the third the verified figure:
  "Your tablet every day: It protects the baby's spine and brain, which form before most women know they are pregnant." ·
  "Your Hb test: Anaemia is common and is easier to fix before pregnancy than during it." · "How long it usually takes: When
  the woman is under 40, more than 8 in 10 couples get pregnant within a year of regular sex without contraception, and more
  than 9 in 10 within two years." New words: the labels "Your tablet every day:", "Your Hb test:", "How long it usually takes:"
  / "रोज़ आपकी गोली:", "आपकी Hb जाँच:", "आम तौर पर कितना समय लगता है:".
- **Questions for the doctor:** is "the iron tablets your doctor prescribes" right for every pregnant woman (the free government
  IFA included)? Is the Hb line right as a benefit of keeping to the plan?

    "Not sure? Tick it. Sakhi keeps your walk easy until your doctor says." / "पक्का नहीं पता? इसे टिक करें। जब तक आपकी डॉक्टर न

    from week 13, and the step for your goal." / "इतनी रफ़्तार से टहलना कि आप बात कर सकें, जो हफ़्तों के साथ बढ़ेगा; 13वें हफ़्ते

    13 and the step for your goal stay. Your doctor decides when you can do more." / "टहलने का कोई लक्ष्य नहीं। 13वें हफ़्ते से

    your next walk, check your answers." / "आपने सखी को एक ऐसे लक्षण के बारे में बताया जो इंतज़ार नहीं कर सकता। अगली बार टहलने से पहले
    अपने जवाब देख लें।"; "Check my answers" / "मेरे जवाब देखें"; "Nothing has changed" / "कुछ नहीं बदला"; My plan's settings:
    "My safety answers" / "मेरे सुरक्षा वाले जवाब".
  - The check-in from 28 weeks: "Your baby’s movements today" / "आज आपके बच्चे की हलचल"; "Your baby’s own pattern, not a
    number." / "आपके बच्चे का अपना ढंग, कोई गिनती नहीं।"; "As usual" / "हमेशा जैसी", "Less than usual" / "आम दिनों से कम", "Not
    sure" / "पक्का नहीं पता", "Not felt any movement today" / "आज कोई हलचल महसूस नहीं हुई"; "Tonight" / "आज रात" (the label over
    the verified side-sleeping line).
  - The step: "Pelvic floor · 1 set" / "पेल्विक फ़्लोर · 1 सेट". The pregnancy card on Home, from 24 weeks: "Baby moving less?
    Get help" / "बच्चे की हलचल कम है? मदद लें".
- **How it works, please check:**
  - Red (no walking target): told to rest or not to exercise, marked high-risk, high BP, Hb under 7, or twins from 28 weeks.
  - Amber (an easy 10 minutes that doesn't grow): sugar in pregnancy or diabetes, problems in an earlier pregnancy, something
    else, not sure, or twins before 28 weeks.
  - Green: the light walk (10, 15, 20 then 30 minutes, growing only after a week of 3 days or more).
  - An earlier C-section and age alone hold nothing, so the screen doesn't ask them.
  - The pelvic floor set from week 13 (and her plan's day 4) in every tier, red included.
  - A yes to "Right now", or "Less than usual", "Not sure" or "Not felt any movement today" in the check-in: no walk until she
    checks her answers again, which Sakhi asks the next day.
  - Her answers again at 14 and 28 weeks.
  - The movements question from 28 weeks, and "Baby moving less? Get help" from 24 weeks, because the kick page's own line
    says to contact the hospital the same day if movements she was feeling slow down or stop.
- **Verified on 20 Oct, word for word (no review):**
  - Help's pregnancy danger line (core's reviewed list) and Help's "Call 112 now" card.
  - The 7 marks of the MCP card copy.
  - The kick page's 2 lines: fewer or weaker movements, get checked today; no movement, hospital now.
  - The symptom guide's sleep line: "From 28 weeks, go to sleep on your side, left or right. A pillow under your belly and
    between your knees helps."
  - The first sentence of the pelvic floor trainer's pregnancy line, as the step's reason. `test/pregplan.mjs` checks each copy
    against its page.
- **Questions for the doctor:**
  - Are the red and amber lists right, and twins red from 28 weeks?
  - Is the pelvic floor set right for every tier, including a stitch, a low-lying placenta or being told to rest?
  - Is 24 weeks right for the "Baby moving less?" button?
  - Is holding the walk after a less-movement answer, until she checks her answers again, right?

### 19.46 Version 39.46: I had a baby, the full plan (MODULE_API section 89)

New lines (draft, waiting for review). The obstetrician reviews every line; the psychologist or psychiatrist the "My baby has
died" lines and the mood question; the women's-health physiotherapist the readiness tests and the squeezes:
  - **"My baby has died":**
    - "We are so sorry";
    - what stops and what stays;
    - "Talk to someone: Tele-MANAS 14416, free, any time.";
    - "Thoughts of harming myself";
    - its crisis line without the baby ("You do not have to go through this alone.");
    - "This page is about feeding a baby.";
    - "Marked this by mistake? Undo".
  - **A warning sign:** "Fever and feeling very unwell": "can be a serious infection. Call 112 or go to hospital now."
  - **Her steps:**
    - "5 gentle squeezes" (during a feed; lying or sitting);
    - "10 squeezes, and the knack" ("Squeeze before you cough, sneeze or lift");
    - "Walk a little, as you feel able" (from the recovery check's reviewed self-care line).
  - **The cards:**
    - "This week: signs in your baby that can't wait" (the list under it is the feeding page's reviewed list);
    - "Your BP check this week";
    - "From your pregnancy": a BP check; the 75 g sugar test, then once a year (from the 6-week check's reviewed list);
    - "Ready to move a little more?" and its 4 answers, with the two checks before starting gently;
    - "Is your body ready for more?", its 6 tests, the bag line, "Stay at this level for 2 more weeks", and "Running, jumping
      and skipping are outside this plan".

    - "Before sex starts again: choose your method";
    - "You can get pregnant before your first period comes back.";
    - the feeding question and its 3 lines. The obstetrician checks "about 4 to 6 weeks" for women who don't breastfeed, and
      the lactational amenorrhoea line.
  - **The check-in:**
    - "This week: any leaks?" (post.js's reviewed leaking tip after it);
    - "How are you in yourself this week?" (the recovery check's own reviewed mood words and crisis card; it ships only after
      the psychologist's review);
    - "Low or worried most days for 2 weeks or more? Talk to your doctor this week, or call Tele-MANAS 14416, free."
  - **The plan's frame:**
    - "Your year, in 5 parts" and its lines;
    - how it works;
    - what happens when;
    - "Your recovery plan has more now";
    - the reveal and Start card lines.

After the bug hunt (MODULE_API 89.9), these lines were added:

### 19.51 Version 39.51: My midlife plan, version 2, "Cool, calm and strong" (MODULE_API section 94)

Mohit, 30 Oct: "start with the midlife plan", then "proceed building with your recommendations" (advice
`docs/midlife-plan-advice-2026-10-30.md`; mockup claude.ai/artifact/4K1ocG3KAXiAtPHQn3rymd). Every line below is new and waits for
review. The Hindi of each line sits beside it in `web/mods/midlife.js` (the chapters) and `web/mods/plan.js` (the plan's lines).
Reused as they are, already reviewed: the POI line (`meno_tl_poi0`), "12 months without a period before 45" (`meno_tl_done_early`),
tonight's card after "Hot flushes woke me" (19.49) and the Learn library's midlife articles (her year's monthly read).

**The chapters** (one a week, 4 a part; the skill on Today that week in brackets). Each ends with "Why this works":
- Cool and calm: "This way of working with thoughts and habits is called CBT. In studies, women who learnt it found hot flushes and
  night sweats less of a problem, even when they still came. The UK’s NICE guideline recommends it, beside hormone therapy or on its
  own."
- A calmer mind: "This way of working with thoughts and habits is called CBT. The UK’s NICE guideline recommends it for low mood
  around menopause."
- Rest: "These rules come from CBT for sleep (CBT for insomnia), which doctors recommend first for sleep problems that last."
- Strong and steady: "Strength and balance work is what doctors and physiotherapists advise after menopause, for muscles, bones and
  fewer falls."

**h1 · What a hot flush is** (Today’s skill: “notice it, name it”)
- As your periods change or stop, your body’s thermostat gets more sensitive. A small rise in warmth sets off a quick cool-down: heat in your face and chest, sweating, a fast heartbeat. Then it passes, often in a few minutes.
- A flush is uncomfortable, but it isn’t dangerous. How much it gets in your way depends partly on what you think and do while it happens, and that part you can change.
- When a flush starts, tell yourself: “This is a flush. It will pass.” Notice where you were and what you were thinking. Tap Hot flushes in your diary that day.

**h2 · Thoughts that turn up the heat** (Today’s skill: “swap the thought”)
- When a flush starts, a thought often comes with it: “Everyone can see.” “I can’t bear this.” Thoughts like these make it feel bigger and harder to sit through.
- “Everyone can see” → “Most people don’t notice. If they do, it’s only a flush.”
- “I can’t bear this” → “I’ve got through every one so far. It will pass.”
- “It will never stop” → “It passes, usually in a few minutes.”
- “Something is wrong with me” → “My body is cooling down. It isn’t dangerous.”
- Pick the calmer thought that fits you best, and say it to yourself each time a flush starts.

**h3 · Carry on, and keep cool** (Today’s skill: “carry on”)
- Rushing out of the room or stopping everything can make a flush feel like an emergency. Staying with what you’re doing, as best you can, teaches your mind that it isn’t one.
- Some things help you feel cooler: a layer you can take off, cotton clothes, cool water, a fan. Notice what sets your flushes off, such as a hot drink, spicy food or a warm room. These make you more comfortable; they don’t stop the flushes.
- When a flush comes: name it, say your calmer thought, breathe slowly, and carry on with what you were doing.

**h4 · Night sweats** (Today’s skill: “your night plan”)
- Night sweats are hot flushes while you sleep. Waking up hot can make you worry about tomorrow, and the worry keeps you awake.
- Before bed: a cool room, light cotton clothes, water by your bed. If a flush wakes you, push back the covers, sip some water and tell yourself: “It will pass. I can rest.”
- Set up your bedroom the cool way every night this week. Can’t get back to sleep after about 20 minutes? Get up and do something calm until you feel sleepy.
- See a doctor this week
- Night sweats with a fever, a cough for more than 2 weeks, or weight loss: see a doctor and ask about a TB test, which is free at government centres.

**q1 · Mood in midlife** (Today’s skill: “notice your mood”)
- Changing hormones, poor sleep and a busy life can all bring worry, irritability or low days in midlife. It’s common, and it can be helped.
- What you think, how you feel and what you do are linked. Change one, and the others often follow.
- Once a day, stop and notice: how am I feeling, and what am I thinking? Just notice. You don’t have to fix it.
- Thoughts of harming yourself? Call Tele-MANAS on 14416: free, any time. In danger right now, call 112.

**q2 · Worry thoughts** (Today’s skill: “a kinder answer”)
- Worry often jumps to the worst: “Something bad will happen.” “I can’t cope.”
- Ask yourself: what would I tell a friend who thought this? Then tell yourself the same.
- When a worry comes, say it or write it down, then the kinder answer you would give a friend.

**q3 · One thing that lifts you** (Today’s skill: “one thing that lifts you”)
- When we feel low, we do less, and doing less makes us feel lower. Small things you enjoy, or that matter to you, turn this around.
- A walk in the morning light, a call to a friend, music, prayer, cooking something you like, time with family.
- Each day, plan one small thing that lifts you, and do it, even if you don’t feel like it at first.

**q4 · Sleep and mood** (Today’s skill: “your wind-down”)
- Poor sleep and low mood feed each other. A steady get-up time and a calm last hour before bed help both.
- Every evening this week, take 20 minutes to wind down before bed: lights low, your phone away, slow breaths or yoga nidra.
- Talk to your doctor
- Low mood or worry on most days for 2 weeks, or it stops you doing your day: talk to your doctor. Talking therapy and medicines help.

**r1 · One get-up time** (Today’s skill: “up at the same time”)
- Your body clock likes a steady morning. Getting up at the same time every day, even after a bad night or on a holiday, makes sleep come more easily at night.
- Lying in after a bad night feels kind, but it pushes tonight’s sleep later.
- Pick your get-up time, and keep it every day this week.

**r2 · Bed is for sleep** (Today’s skill: “the 20-minute rule”)
- Lying awake for a long time teaches your body that bed is a place to worry.
- Go to bed when you feel sleepy, not just because it’s late. Can’t sleep after about 20 minutes? Get up, sit somewhere dim and do something calm. Go back when you feel sleepy.
- Use the 20-minute rule this week, every night you need it.

**r3 · Light, naps and tea** (Today’s skill: “morning light”)
- Daylight in the morning sets your body clock. A walk outside, or sitting by a window after you get up, helps.
- If you nap, keep it to about 20 minutes, before 3 pm. Have your tea or coffee before 2 pm.
- Get 15 minutes of morning light every day this week.

**r4 · When sleep needs a doctor** (Today’s skill: “a calm last hour”)
- Keep your last hour before bed calm: lights low, your phone away, and Wind down if you like.
- Tell your doctor if
- You snore loudly and stop breathing for a moment, feel very sleepy in the day, or have slept badly on most nights for 3 months. These can be treated: a short course of talking therapy for sleep (CBT for insomnia) helps many women, and doctors try it before sleeping pills.
- Keep a calm last hour every evening this week.

**s1 · Muscle after 40** (Today’s skill: “steady on your feet”)
- After menopause, muscles and bones get weaker faster. Strength work twice a week keeps your muscles strong, and helps your bones too.
- You don’t need a gym: a chair, a wall and your own body are enough.
- Every day: Steady on your feet, 3 minutes. Twice a week: Strength at home.

**s2 · Steady on your feet** (Today’s skill: “steady on your feet”)
- Good balance helps prevent falls, and most broken bones later in life come from a fall.
- A few minutes of balance practice a day makes a difference. Always keep a hand near a wall or a chair.
- Steady on your feet, every day this week.

**s3 · Lift and bend the safe way** (Today’s skill: “steady on your feet”)
- To lift something: bend your knees and hips, keep your back tall, hold it close to you, and don’t twist while you lift.
- Avoid bending forward with something heavy, and sudden twists, most of all if your doctor has said your bones are weak.
- This week, lift the safe way every time: a bucket, a child, the shopping.

**s4 · Food for strong bones** (Today’s skill: “steady on your feet”)
- Calcium foods every day: milk or curd, paneer, ragi, til, green leafy vegetables, rajma and chana.
- Protein at each meal helps your muscles: dal, paneer and curd, and eggs, fish or chicken if you eat them.
- Calcium or vitamin D tablets: only if your doctor advises them.
- Every day this week: a calcium food, some protein at each meal, and Steady on your feet.

**The plan's lines:**
- Today's step: "Today’s skill: {the chapter's skill}" · "2 minutes · from this week’s chapter" · "This week’s sleep rule" · "This
  week’s chapter" · "This month’s read".
- Its reasons (behind "Why?"): "A skill you practise every day makes hot flushes easier to handle, even when they still come." ·
  "Noticing your thoughts, and doing one thing that lifts you, helps your mood day by day." · "A steady body clock lets sleep come
  more easily." · "A few minutes of balance a day helps keep you steady on your feet."
- Reworded for every midlife plan: `plan_fb_hot` "Cooling down and a calm breath can make a hot flush more comfortable while it
  passes." (was "Cooling down and slow breathing can make hot flushes easier to get through"; The Menopause Society 2023 doesn't
  recommend paced breathing or cooling as a flush treatment) · `plan_fb_bones` "Strength work two days a week keeps your muscles
  strong after 40, and helps your bones too."
- Steady on your feet (3 minutes): "One leg at the wall: Stand side-on to a wall with one hand on it. Lift one foot a little off the
  floor and hold, up to 30 seconds." · "Heel to toe at the wall: Walk slowly along a wall with one hand on it,
  one foot right in front of the other, heel to toe. 10 steps, then turn and come back." · Heel raises, as in Strength at home.
- "Your day": "Has a doctor said your bones are weak, or did a bone break after a small fall?" (Yes, No, Not sure) · "Chest pain,
  fainting, or getting very breathless when you walk?" (Yes, No).
- Under a yes, and on My plan: "Please see your doctor before you do more. Until then your walk stays easy, and Gentle start takes the
  place of Strength at home. Chest pain right now, or pain spreading to your jaw or arm: call 112." · "Every move in this plan is
  bone-safe, with no jumps. Ask your doctor about a bone scan and tablets."
- Her weekly number (the check-in, once a week from day 7): "This week, how much did {hot flushes and night sweats / poor sleep /
  worry or low mood / aches, stiffness or tiredness} get in your way?" 0 · not at all to 10 · very much.
- Her results: "How much {…} got in your way: {a} of 10 in your first 2 weeks, {b} of 10 in your last 2." · "Nights you slept well or
  okay: {a} of {n} in your first 2 weeks, {b} of {m} in your last 2." · "Strength days: {n}".
- Cards: "Your periods stopped before 45" (with the reviewed line) · weeks 4 to 7: "Hot flushes still getting in your way? The most
  effective treatment for hot flushes is hormone therapy, prescribed by an MBBS gynaecologist. Your plan works beside it, or on its
  own." · for her other goals, only with hot flushes in her diary, aches and tiredness, or hormone medicine: "Your doctor can help

  "You take hormone medicine: keep to your doctor’s plan, and see your doctor about it once a year." with "Call 112 now if one calf is

  tiredness: ask your doctor which blood tests you need, often Hb and thyroid." · weeks 12 to 16: "Your checks after 40. After 40,
